2026. 11.10
先週（11/2〜11/9）発表の主な経済指標
⽶国雇⽤は安定も、AI曝露度の⾼い職業で若年雇⽤が弱含
み。雇⽤不安が⺠主党躍進を後押しした可能性。AI関連株が
調整するなか、韓国中銀は投資減速のアジアへの波及を警戒
所定内給与は物価上昇率を上回る伸びが持続。冬季ボーナス
も押し上げ。電気・ガス料⾦補助再開は消費の追い⾵に。⼀⽅、
消費回復が進めば基調的な物価上昇圧⼒を強める可能性
注目点② 賃上げの波及と物価対策が日本の消費を下支え注目点① 米AI投資拡大の裏で広がる「不安」と「リスク」
⾮農業部⾨雇⽤者数は市場予想を上回り、失業
率も低下。ISM景況指数は製造業・⾮製造業とも
に拡⼤圏を維持。雇⽤・企業活動の底堅さの⼀⽅
で、消費者マインドは持ち直すも依然として低⽔準

（出所）米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
失業率
米国：雇用統計は良好な結果。一方で、賃金上昇もインフレ見合いで見劣り
 10月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋16.5万人と市場予想（同＋6.5万人）を大きく上回る結果
―過 去2カ月も合わせて8.4万人の上方修正。業種別では専門ビジネスサービスやヘルスケアが全体をけん引
― 時間当たり賃金も前月比＋0.4％（前年比＋3.1％）と着実に増加。ただしインフレ率に割り負けている状況が継続
 失業率は4.1％と前月（4.2％）から小幅低下も、失業期間は長期化
― 失業期間の中央値は11.9週（9月：同11.5週）と長期化。27週以上の長期失業者も増加基調維持。企業の採用率は低下

傾向が続いており、労働者が失業状態から脱しにくい傾向が継続
3
（出所）米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
非農業部門雇用者数

雇用者数は市場予想を大きく上回る
過去2カ月もそれぞれ上方修正

（年）
米国：AI普及の影響は若年層に偏在。雇用機会への不安が拡大
 AI曝露度別の雇用指数をみると、22～34歳では曝露度の高い職業の雇用が相対的に弱含み
― AIの影響がキャリア形成段階の若年層に偏っている可能性
 若年層では、AIによる雇用代替に加え、物価高など生活面の不安も根強い状況
― AIを否定的に捉える割合も若い世代ほど高く、AI普及への警戒感がうかがえる状況

4
（注） 上図の若年層とは18～29歳。経済状況は回答者の主観的な評価
（出所） Harvard Youth Poll、ABC News、Washington Post、Ipsosより、みずほ総合研
究所調査部作成
AI曝露度別雇用指数
年齢別AIの認識：「AIは社会に悪い」と回答した割合
（注） 1．AI曝露度とは各職業の業務が生成AIの影響を受ける度合いを示す。職業を曝露度
に応じて5分位に分類し、低・中・高はそれぞれ第1・第3・第5分位を指す
2．年齢層別の雇用指数は、米国雇用統計の家計調査における年齢別雇用者数をウェ
イトに加重平均して算出。年齢区分が一致しない部分は、各階級内の雇用者数が均
等に分布すると仮定して按分
（出所）スタンフォード大学デジタル経済研究所、ADP Researchより、みずほ総合研究所調査
部作成
中堅・シニア層（35歳以上）
若年層（22～34歳）
若年層が感じる経済的脅威
若年層ほどAIへの反感が強い
若年層に偏る雇用への影響

（年/月）
米国：若年層の経済的不安が中間選挙の投票行動にも影響か
 2026年中間選挙では「物価高」や「雇用」への不安が若年層の主要な関心事項に
― こうした経済的不安が共和党政権への不満につながった可能性
― 年齢別の投票動向では、若年層を中心に民主党支持が優勢。生活費負担や将来の雇用機会への不安が投票行動に
影響した模様
― なお、議会でも世代交代が進展。若い世代の政策選好が今後の政策形成に与える影響にも注目

### Page 7

（参考）民主党が掲げる主な政策
 勝利した民主党は、若年層の不満とも重なる生活費負担の軽減（アフォーダビリティ）を共通の政策課題に位置付け
 具体的な政策手段を巡っては、中道派と左派・進歩派の間で違い
― 中道派は競争促進や規制緩和、財政規律を重視する一方、左派・進歩派は大企業への規制・課税強化や政府支出の
拡大を主張。今後の党内調整が焦点に
6

（年）
（2020＝100）
アジア：AI投資減速の影響が域内の貿易ネットワークを通じて波及するリスク
 韓国中銀はAI関連投資の拡大に伴う過剰投資やレバレッジの高まり、循環金融などの金融リスクを警戒
― AI産業の期待収益の過大評価や最終的なリスク所在把握が困難になり、リスクが過小評価される可能性があると指摘
― また、米国企業のAI投資が減速する悲観シナリオでは、韓国の半導体輸出が大幅に減少し、GDP成長率を0.4％Pt押し
下げると試算
― 韓国のAI関連財貿易は中国・台湾との取引が4割超。アジア域内で形成された貿易ネットワークを通じて、AI投資減速の
影響が広範に波及する可能性

日本：物価対策の再開が家計負担を軽減。消費の回復を後押し
9
 政府は冬場の電気・ガス料金補助の再開を決定。標準的な家庭で計7,000円程度の負担軽減を見込む
― 一方、ガソリン価格の補助は段階的に縮小（基準額を毎月5円ずつ引き上げ）。電気・ガス料金補助金の効果を一部減殺
 補助金を巡る政策変更は、コアCPIの前年比を最大0.4％程度押し下げ
― 賃金上昇に加え、政策による物価負担軽減が家計の実質購買力を下支え
― 物価対策がCPIを押し下げる一方、消費回復を通じて基調的な物価上昇圧力が強まる可能性には留意が必要

（注） ガソリンと同額の補助を実施している軽油・重油・灯油、ガソリン補助額の4割相当
の支援を実施している航空機燃料については未定

欧州：10月消費者物価はエネルギー主導で加速。企業は販売価格の引き上げを計画
10
 10月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋4.0％と、エネルギー主導で前月（同＋3.8％）から加速
― 一方、コアは同＋2.5％と前月から不変。エネルギー高の二次的な影響はいまだ顕在化せず
 コスト上昇圧力が高まるなか、企業は販売価格の引き上げを計画。高止まりするインフレにECBは警戒姿勢を維持
― 企業の販売価格見通しは7～9月期に上昇。カジミール・スロバキア中銀総裁は、過去数カ月にわたるコスト上昇圧力の

中国：政策金利をリバースレポ翌日物に変更。銀行貸出金利の新たな決定メカニズムを模索
 中国人民銀行は政策金利をリバースレポ翌日物に変更（11/2）。短期市場金利の誘導精度を高める狙い
― リバースレポ7日物から切り替え、翌日物のオペを毎営業日実施。最優遇貸出金利（LPR）も翌日物金利を参照へ
― インターバンク市場における債券担保付レポ翌日物金利（DR001）が誘導目標と明言。短期金利の安定化を図る考え
 銀行貸出金利の新たな決定メカニズムを模索。DR001を参照して貸出金利を決定する方法を試験的に導入

― 背景には、銀行貸出の指標金利であるLPRが「最優遇」から乖離し、LPRより低利での貸出比率が上昇している実態
― 今後、財務力のある企業は銀行と協議の上でDR001かLPRを選択し、小規模企業や個人には引き続きLPRを適用へ

中国：景況感は再び悪化し、2カ月ぶりの節目割れ。製造業は需給ともに振るわず
 10月の景況感は再び悪化。総合PMIは49.7と前月（50.7）から大幅に低下し、2カ月ぶりの節目割れ
― うち非製造業PMIが49.7と前月（50.2）から低下も、宿泊やスポーツ・娯楽が持ち直し、情報サービスは堅調だった
― 建設業PMIは48.0と前月（50.3）から急低下。中国物流購買聯合会は建設工事の着工が遅れ気味と指摘
 製造業PMIは49.3と前月（50.1）から低下。供給側の生産、需要側の受注がともに振るわず

― 生産が49.8（前月：51.7）、新規受注が49.4（同：50.5）、新規輸出受注も48.7（同：50.0）といずれも節目の50を下回る
― 中東情勢に伴う価格上昇に一服感。原材料価格は56.8（同：60.8）、出荷価格は51.7（同：54.0）まで戻す

中国：PPIは生産財が急上昇も、川下への価格転嫁進まず。 CPIは食品価格が下押し要因に
 10月のPPIは前年比＋5.7％（前月：同＋5.0％）。中東情勢の影響による資源・エネルギー関連業種の価格上昇続く
― 石油・石炭加工（同＋29.9％）と非鉄金属加工（同＋18.6％）、化学品（同＋16.4％）が引き続き高い伸び
― 川上の生産財が同＋7.4％まで上昇も、川下の消費財は同▲0.1％と下落継続。価格転嫁は一向に進まず
 CPIは同＋0.7％と、前月（同＋0.9％）から減速。食品価格の下落（▲2.9％）が下押し要因に（▲0.49％Pt）

― 他方、燃料価格高騰により、ガソリンは同＋22.4％（前月：同＋16.6％）まで上昇し、全体を＋0.63％Pt押し上げ
― 食品・エネルギーを除くコアは同＋0.8％（前月：＋1.0％）と弱いが、通信機器はメモリー高騰で同＋16.1％の伸び

### Page 15

アジア：ASEAN5の製造業景況感は概ね堅調。原油高など仕入コスト上昇の影響は残存
 ASEAN5の10月の製造業景況感は、フィリピンを除き改善圏。受注・生産は好調も、コスト高が継続
― タイ、ベトナムは高水準で推移し、インドネシアは回復が鮮明。一方、フィリピンが基準となる50を下回る水準
― S&Pはリリース資料において、新規受注の増加が生産を支えたものの、仕入コスト上昇と原料調達・物流の制約を指摘
 ASEAN5では、生産者物価（PPI）が高止まり。原油高の影響が残存
― 原油高が続く中、石炭・石油に加えて化学やプラ・ゴム等の製品価格にも上昇圧力が継続
― 仕入コストの高止まりが、企業収益や生産活動の重荷となるリスクに留意が必要
14

 ASEAN5の一部の国で財政収支が悪化
― ベトナム、マレーシア、インドネシアで改善した一方で、フィリピンとタイで財政赤字が拡大し、明暗が分かれる結果に
― フィリピンは税収の伸び悩み、タイは景気下支えに向けた公共投資拡大や予算執行の加速などにより歳出が大きく増加
 各国とも財政面の制約を抱え、追加的な景気下支え余地は限定的
― タイは公的債務残高が法定上限（GDP比70％）に接近。マレーシアも政府債務残高が高水準
― フィリピンは財政赤字の縮小が課題。インドネシアは財政赤字上限（GDP比3％）が制約要因

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 27 日
上週重點訊息
◼ AI 投資回報疑慮升溫，資金轉向債市避險：市場對大型科技企業 AI 基礎建設投資規模、融資
需求及未來獲利回收能力的疑慮升溫，導致科技股賣壓擴大，10 月 22 日 Nasdaq 指數下跌
約 2.6%。 隨著市場風險偏好下降 ， 避險資金轉向美國公債 ，10 年期公債殖利率單日下降約 19
個基點，創 2023 年 3 月以來最大單日跌幅，並於 23 日回升至 4.99%。市場關注焦點逐漸由
AI 投資規模轉向資本支出效益，後續大型科技企業財報及現金流表現，將成為評估 AI 產業評

價合理性的重要依據。
◼ 中東衝突推升通膨風險：中東衝突持續升溫，伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝攻擊沙烏地阿拉伯，引
發市場對原油供應中斷的擔憂，推升布蘭特原油價格於10 月 23 日升至每桶 116.72 美元，單
週上漲約 6.2%，創 2022 年以來最高週收盤水準。能源價格攀升加劇全球通膨壓力，並壓抑
市場風險偏好。
◼ 法國政治動盪加劇財政疑慮，歐洲主權債風險重新受到關注：法國因生活成本上升爆發大規模
抗議，進一步加深市場對政府財政整頓能力的疑慮。10 月 19 日法國 10 年期公債殖利率上升
約 10 個基點至 4.88%附近，與德國公債利差擴大至約 144 個基點，創 2012 年以來最高收盤
水準 ， 法股單日下跌約1.5%， 歐元同步走弱 。 市場擔憂政治不確定性可能削弱財政改革進度 ，
並透過主權債評價影響銀行業資產負債表。後續須關注法國財政政策、歐洲主權債利差及銀行
融資成本變化。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線宜審慎，中期聚焦基本面穩健的 AI 企業：近期 AI 資本支出、融資需求及評價調整

使科技股波動加劇 ， 但先進製程與半導體設備需求仍具韌性 ，AI 中長期成長趨勢未改變 。 隨市
場焦點由成長題材轉向投資效益，企業營收與訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率的重要性提
升 ， 具技術優勢 、 財務穩健及獲利能見度的企業相對有利 ， 而高度依賴外部融資者宜審慎看待 。
◼ 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：市場風險偏好降溫時，美國公債仍具一定
避險效果。儘管 Fed 貨幣政策路徑與能源價格增添利率不確定性，短天期債券易受政策利率
牽動，超長天期債券面臨財政赤字、國債供給及期限溢價等壓力，相較之下，中天期美債的風
險報酬較為均衡。信用債方面，投資等級企業基本面及現金流仍具韌性，後續仍可持續關注。

上週重點訊息
◼ Fed 維持利率不變，兩位委員主張升息：美國聯準會於 10 月 28 日以 10 比 2 的表決結果，
將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持在 3.75%至 4%，其中理事庫克與克里夫蘭聯準銀行總裁哈瑪
克對該決議投下反對票，並主張應升息 1 碼。Fed 在聲明中表示，經濟活動正以強勁步調擴
張，但通膨仍然偏高。在已有兩位委員主張升息的情況下，後續通膨數據將是 Fed 12 月是
否升息的重要影響因素。
◼ Alphabet 自由現金流轉負幅度超出預期：Alphabet 於 10 月 28 日公布第三季財報，自由現

金流為負 90.7 億美元，明顯低於分析師預期的負 27 億美元，同時 Alphabet 維持全年
1,950 億至 2,050 億美元的資本支出指引，顯示 AI 基礎建設投資仍持續消耗公司現金。
Alphabet 該季自由現金流明顯轉負，也引發市場對於科技巨頭資本支出的疑慮，並帶動整體
科技類股出現修正。
◼ 英特爾因 PC 需求疲弱下修第四季財測：英特爾於 10 月 29 日公布第三季財報，營收年增
21%至 165.1 億美元，高於公司原先財測；其中資料中心與 AI 部門營收年增 78%，為 15 年
來最強勁的成長，但以 PC 為主的客戶端運算部門營收則年減 5%。公司指出，由於記憶體短
缺推升 PC 售價，加上企業 IT 預算受限，使 PC 需求轉弱。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線波動較大，中期看好現金流穩健的 AI 受惠企業：本次財報季顯示雲端營收加速成
長與未認列合約持續擴大，AI 需求仍強；但部分企業的資本支出攀升、自由現金流轉弱，加
上 Fed 可能於 12 月升息，短線科技股波動恐將延續。然而中期而言，AI 產業將由全面成長

轉向基本面分化，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健且 AI 投資已能轉化為獲利的企業，風險報酬相
對有利；對高度依賴外部融資、自由現金流持續為負的企業則宜維持審慎。
◼ 5 至 10 年期美債及投資等級公司債兼顧票息收益與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性，加上政府
與企業發債供給維持高檔，可能使市場利率中期仍處於較高水準，但近期公債殖利率回落，
顯示市場避險需求升溫，支撐債券買盤；整體而言，短天期債券面臨未來利率下降後的再投
資風險，長天期債券則較容易受到公債供給增加、期限溢酬及利率波動影響，5-10 年期美國
公債與投資級債券則較能兼顧息收與利率風險。

資料中心監管疑慮衝擊 AI 供應鏈，美股全面重挫
2026 年 11 月 06 日
一、 市場動態
1. 資料中心監管疑慮引爆 AI 供應鏈賣壓，美股選後漲勢一日反轉：民主黨取得參、眾兩
院多數，美國進入分裂政府格局。S&P 500 於 11 月 4 日先漲 1.58%，11 月 5 日卻重
挫 4.36%，創2025 年 4 月以來最大單日跌幅 ；Nasdaq 跌 5.52%，費半大跌 12.25%，
十一大產業全數收黑。主因有三：一是資料中心開發成為州級政策議題，德州州長當
選人主張暫停新建，全美新增容量約三成可能受影響；二是高通與 Arm 財報不如預

期；三是建設遞延將推高依賴外部融資業者的持有成本。費半重挫前已自 6 月高點回
落近三成，政策不確定性進一步放大跌幅（詳見表一）。

可透過電價分攤、建設許可及稅務優惠影響新案進度，UBS 估計 2028 年全美新增資
料中心容量約三成可能受影響。國會對科技巨頭與資料中心用電的監督亦可能增強，

生於上線前，若電力、許可或工程延後，收入認列隨之推遲，利息與持有成本卻須持
續負擔。儘管 12 月升息機率降至約 65%，借款成本仍高，依賴外部融資或單一客戶
合約的 AI 基礎建設業者將面臨回報壓縮與再融資風險。
4. 科技股在本次重挫前已明顯修正 ， 評價落至極低水準：費半自 6 月 22 日高點 14,634.72
點已回落逾 30%，Nasdaq 亦自 10 月 6 日高點 27,599.89 點回落逾 10%。市場質疑
AI 投資效益，降低對高評價企業的容忍度，政策不確定性因而放大修正。近期跌幅較
屬估值下調而非基本面惡化，若晶片訂單與獲利預估維持穩定，市場有望回溫。

三、 市場看法
1. 短期評價修正而非長期趨勢反轉，AI 資本循環仍處擴張階段：我們視本波為多頭格局
中的健康修正。修正反映股東對龐大資本承諾要求更高風險補償，而非 AI 變現失速：
Google Cloud 第三季營收年增 103%、 營業利益年增209%，四大雲端業者資本支出
預估上修至約 1.16 兆美元，顯示企業看見更大市場而主動加碼（詳見表四）。此外，
政策衝擊集中於州與地方，聯邦仍支持資料中心擴建，選舉結果本質是要求用電成本
內部化，而非否定 AI 投資。

《Ratepayer Protection Act》亦顯示跨黨派共識；（二）雲端業者資本支出指引未下
修、未見取消訂單；（三）11 月 10 日 CPI 及 13 日 PPI 物價月增率回落；（四）財政
部維持附息債標售規模，資金回流市場；（五）VIX 回落且投資等級債利差穩定。訊號
出現後，市場有望回歸基本面。 
3. 政策先影響建設速度，AI 需求仍須以訂單驗證：州級限制首先改變的是資料中心選址
與交付時程，而非 AI 需求本身。雲端客戶若維持支出計畫，專案可調整順序或轉往他
州，惟設備交付遞延將壓抑供應商近季營收；聯邦立法限制則須經國會及總統否決權，
短期落地難度高。若主要客戶訂單穩定，本次修正較屬建設時程遞延的風險重定價。 
4. 新增算力面臨遞延，既有算力相對有利：高盛估計可能收緊開發限制的州約占美國規
劃中資料中心容量的 45%，其中德州約占 25%。新案放慢下，大型雲端業者既有算力
可望受惠於利用率與議價能力提升，資本支出延後亦減輕現金流壓力；仍待併網的專
案及高槓桿業者則面臨回收遞延，產業鏈分化將擴大。 
5. 歷史經驗顯示選後股市多正向，後續表現仍宜留意基本面變化：彭博統計 1950 年以
來，美國期中選舉後 12 個月 S&P 500 平均報酬約 15%，19 次全數收正，最高為 1954
年的逾 30%，最弱的 1986 年亦小幅上漲。府會分治抑制政策大幅轉向為選後行情主
要支撐，惟後續表現仍須觀察基本面(詳見下圖)。

6. 企業獲利仍強勁成長，本次修正以估值與風險溢價重估為主：彭博 11 月 4 日統計，
S&P 500 已公布財報公司中 79.85%優於預期，資訊科技更達 92.45%，第三季 EPS
成長率分別為 29.0%及 67.8%（詳見表五）。財報為美股提供中期支撐，短期評價則
取決於 2027 年獲利預估能否承受建設遞延與融資成本壓力。 
表五：S&P 500 第三季財報統計（截至 2026/11/04） 
類別 已公布家數 優於預期家數 優於預期比率 第三季 EPS 成長率

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕

資料來源：Bloomberg；元大銀行整理
7. 最新經濟數據仍具韌性，聯準會利率路徑仍具變數：初領失業救濟金降至 18.9 萬人，
第三季勞動生產力季增年率 3.6%、單位勞動成本季減 0.7%，經濟仍具韌性。惟油價
逼近每桶 100 美元，若通膨高於預期，升息定價恐再升溫，壓抑科技股估值與長債價
格。
8. 信用市場是判斷風險是否由股權評價擴散至金融條件的重要指標：若 AI 企業持續擴大
資本支出與外部融資，償債能力將受關注。大型投資等級科技企業現金流穩定，較能

吸收壓力；高槓桿或依賴單一客戶的 AI 基礎建設業者則較易受利差上升衝擊。信用利
差若維持穩定，風險仍以股權評價調整為主；若同步擴大，則須警惕金融條件收緊。
四、 投資建議：
1. 短期修正不改 AI 長期趨勢 ， 切勿恐慌殺低錯殺優質標的：本次重挫源自選後政策不確
定性與評價重新定價，AI 需求與企業獲利尚未反轉，屬多頭中的短期波動。應避免恐
慌出清而錯殺優質標的；已持有者以續抱為主，加碼宜分批逢低，避免高槓桿操作。
2. 以股債平衡穿越短期波動，等待基本面重新主導市場：我們認為，本次修正屬短期政
策衝擊與評價調整，中長期科技股展望仍正面。在政策與通膨明朗前，建議將集中度
過高的科技股部位，適度轉向波動較低的平衡型或多重資產商品，搭配優質債券穩定
收益，並保留股票部位分批布局優質標的，待反彈訊號確認後再提高風險資產比重。
3. 股票部位聚焦優質、評價回落且前景較佳的產業：修正後部分優質企業評價已回到合
理區間，股票建議聚焦：一、握有長期電力合約、已通電園區的大型雲端業者；二、
明年展望佳的 AI 供應鏈，如 ASIC、光通訊、電力設備、半導體設備及資安；三、跌

幅較小的軟體及非科技優質股。高度仰賴新建資料中心與外部融資的業者，宜待環境
明朗再評估。
4. 中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債仍較具優勢：在美國政策風險升溫及科技股評價
調整期間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源價格的不確定性，現
階段不建議延長存續期間。中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債在兼顧票息收益、利
率敏感度與資產配置穩定性上仍較具優勢。信用債方面，營運現金流充足、槓桿適中
及再融資需求可控的發行人仍較為穩健，對AI 相關企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合
約現金流與債務到期結構。

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月消費統計 
サービスを中心に消費全体が弱く、前月から減少

経済調査部 エコノミスト 龐 鈞文

[要約] 
◼ 2026年9月の家計調査における二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は前月比▲2.8％と3カ月
ぶりに減少した。複数の需要側統計を用いて補正した世帯消費動向指数（ CTIミクロ）
で見ても実質消費は同▲1.6％と2カ月連続で減少した。また、供給側統計の1つであ
る商業動態統計では、CPIの財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.0％だった。総じ
て見れば、9月の個人消費は前月から減少したと判断される。 
◼ 先行きの個人消費は底堅く推移するとみている。名目賃金は最低賃金の引き上げもあ
って上昇が続く見込みである。一方、政府の物価高対策が予定されるものの、原油高
などによる幅広い分野への価格上昇の波及、円安や人件費上昇による価格転嫁などを
通じて個人消費を下押しする可能性には引き続き注意が必要だ。

＜2026年9月の消費総括＞サービスを中心に消費全体が弱い 
需要側統計である家計調査における二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は、前月比▲2.8％と3カ月
ぶりに減少した。複数の需要側統計を用いて補正した世帯消費動向指数（CTIミクロ）で見て
も実質消費は同▲1.6％と 2カ月連続で減少した（図表 1）。また、供給側統計の 1つである商
業動態統計では、CPIの財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.0％だった。総じて見れば、
2026年 9月の個人消費は前月から減少したと判断される。

＜CTIミクロ・家計調査（需要側）＞全ての費目が減少 
2026年 9月の CTIミクロ（二人以上の世帯）を費目別に見ると、10大費目1のうち、「教養娯
楽」（前月比▲3.3％）、「教育」（同▲6.5％）、「食料」（同▲0.9％）、「光熱・水道」（同▲
3.1％）、「家具・家事用品」（同▲ 4.8％）、「交通・通信」（同▲0.8％）、「保健医療」（同
▲2.4％）、「住居」 （同▲1.6％） 、「被服及び履物」 （同▲3.0％） 、「その他」 （同▲0.4％）の全
ての費目が減少した（図表 2）。全ての費目が減少したのは消費税率が 10％に引き上げられた
2019年 10月以来だ。 
図表 2：実質世帯消費動向指数（CTIミクロ）の前月比

続いて、CTIミクロの10大費目に含まれる個別品目への支出について確認する。「教養娯楽」
は2カ月連続で減少した。教養娯楽用品や教養娯楽耐久財を中心に支出が減少した。「教育」は
3カ月連続で減少した。授業料等を中心に支出が減少した2。「食料」は3カ月ぶりに減少した。
飲料を中心に、穀類などへの支出が減少した。「光熱・水道」は2カ月ぶりに減少した。電気代
を中心に、上下水道料などへの支出が減少した。「家具・家事用品」は2カ月連続で減少した。
家庭用耐久財を中心に、家事雑貨などへの支出が減少した。「交通・通信」は2カ月連続で減少
した。自動車等関係費や通信を中心に支出が減少した。「保健医療」は3カ月連続で減少した。
保健医療用品・器具を中心に支出が減少した。「住居」は2カ月ぶりに減少した。設備修繕・維
持を中心に支出が減少した。「被服及び履物」は2カ月連続で減少した。シャツ・セーター類や
他の被服を中心に支出が減少した。「その他」は2カ月連続で減少した。諸雑費を中心に支出が
減少した。 
消費支出を財・サービス別に見ると（大和総研による季節調整値、図表3中段右）、サービス
（前月比▲3.9％）が2カ月連続で減少し、全体を押し下げた。非耐久財（同▲1.6％）は2カ月
ぶりに、耐久財（同▲3.6％）は 5カ月連続で、半耐久財（同▲3.0％）は 2カ月連続で、いず
れも減少した。 
家計調査における基礎的支出は前月比▲0.6％と2カ月ぶりに減少した。また、選択的支出は
同▲7.6％と 3カ月ぶりに大幅に減少した（いずれも大和総研による季節調整値、図表 3中段
左）。

2 ただし、授業料等は子どもや学生がいる一部の世帯以外はほとんど支出しないため、サンプルサイズが小さ
く振れが大きい。

＜商業動態統計（供給側）＞小売販売額は名目・実質ともに2カ月連続で減少 
2026年 9月の商業動態統計によると、名目小売販売額は前月比▲0.7％と 2カ月連続で減少
した（図表 4、5） 。また、CPIの財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.0％だった。 
名目小売販売額を業種別に見ると、7業種中 4 業種が前月から減少した。「その他小売業」
（前月比▲2.1％）、「燃料小売業」 （同▲2.7％）ともに 2カ月連続で減少した。「機械器具小売
業」 （同▲2.3％）も 2カ月連続で減少した。家電大型専門店の販売額（大和総研による季節調
整値） （同▲3.2％）などが減少した。「飲食料品小売業」 （同▲0.2％）は 2カ月連続で減少し
た。コンビニのファーストフード及び日配食品（同▲2.3％）と加工食品（同▲1.9％）が減少
した。 
他方、3業種が前月から増加した。「自動車小売業」 （前月比＋0.1％）は 5カ月ぶりにプラス
に転じた。9月の新車販売台数（大和総研による季節調整値）は同＋2.0％と増加しており3、
この結果とも整合的だ。「織物・衣服・身の回り品小売業」 （同＋4.2％）は2カ月ぶりに増加し
た。気温が低下したこと（巻末図表「全国の平均気温・日照時間・降水量」）や休日数が多か
ったことなどにより、秋物商品の販売が好調 だったとみられる。「各種商品小売業」（同＋
2.1％）は 2カ月ぶりに増加した。スーパーの商品販売額（同＋1.0％）と百貨店の商品販売額
（同＋0.5％）が増加した。

＜2026年10月の消費＞9月から減少を見込む 
業界統計や個社データ、JCB消費NOWをもとに判断すると、2026年10月の消費は9月から減
少したとみている4。財消費では、10月前半の実績をもとに試算した家電のJCB消費額（大和総
研による季節調整値） 、コンビニのJCB消費額（同） 、新車販売台数（同）とアパレルの売上高
は減少した。一方、百貨店の売上高は堅調に推移した。サービス消費では、宿泊の JCB消費額
（同）は前月から減少した一方、外食のJCB消費額（同）は増加した。

＜先行き＞個人消費は底堅く推移する見込み／価格上昇の広がりに注意 
先行きの個人消費は底堅く推移するとみている。名目賃金の高い伸びが下支えとなるだろう。
ただし、原油高の波及や円安などによる物価上昇が実質賃金を押し下げ、消費の重荷となる可
能性には注意が必要だ。 
名目賃金の伸びは高水準で推移するだろう。厚生労働省によると、全ての都道府県で 10～12
月に最低賃金が引き上げられる。2026年度の最低賃金の全国加重平均額は1,177円で5、引き上
げ率は＋5.0％（前年度は＋6.3％）と高水準を維持した。また、企業収益の改善などを背景に、
高水準の年末賞与が期待される。 
消費者物価について、12月使用分から 2027年 2月使用分までの電気・ガス料金の補助とい
った政府の物価高対策が実施される予定だ6。ただし、原油高などにより、原材料費や輸送費の
上昇を通じて幅広い分野の価格に波及し、円安や人件費の高騰などによる価格転嫁も物価を押
し上げ、消費を下押しする可能性には引き続き注意が必要だ。また、帝国データバンクによる
と、飲食料品の 9～10月の大規模な値上げラッシュは一服したが、11月の値上げ品目数は前年
11月の約 10倍規模となり、引き続き高水準で推移している。賃上げや物流費、エネルギーコ
ストの上昇など、食品を取り巻くコスト環境が楽観視できる状況にはないため、値上げは続く
とみている7。 
また、金利上昇による住宅ローン等の利払い負担増が個人消費にもたらす悪影響にも警戒す
る必要がある8。さらに、消費動向調査（内閣府）によると、10月の消費者態度指数（二人以
上の世帯、季節調整値）は 35.0（前月差▲0.4pt）と 2カ月連続で小幅に低下し、依然として
低水準にあり（巻末図表「消費者マインド」参照） 、節約志向の高まりに引き続き注意が必要
だ。

4 詳細は、拙稿「消費データブック（2026/11/5号）」（大和総研レポート、2026年11月 5日）を参照。 
5 厚生労働省「全ての都道府県で地域別最低賃金の改定額が答申されました ～答申での全国加重平均額は昨
年度から 56円引上げの1,177円～」（2026年9月3日） 
6 資源エネルギー庁「電気・ガス料金支援」（2026年11月9日閲覧） 
7 帝国データバンク「『食品主要 195社』価格改定動向調査 ― 2026年 11月 飲食料品値上げ、年間 2万品
目超え 11月は 1477品目 秋の『値上げラッシュ』は一服」（2026年10月 30日） 
8 詳細は、神田慶司・畑中宏仁・中村華奈子・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026年8月」（大和総研レポート、 
2026年8月24日）を参照。

### Page 1

M
Idea
3Q preview: Co-Tech is scheduled to report its 3Q results on November 6. We
estimate 3Q revenue at NT$3,169mn (+13% q/q, +60% y/y), primarily driven by
higher copper prices and product-mix shifts. We lower our 3Q GM estimate from
28.2% to 27.0% (-30bps q/q, +650bps y/y), reflecting the impact of higher
production costs and copper prices during the quarter. Co-Tech has already reported
preliminary monthly profits for August and September at the request of the TWSE

(report link); combined August and September net profit now accounts for
approximately 67% of our 3Q estimate. We estimate 3Q net profit at NT$610mn
(+12% q/q, +141% y/y), implying EPS of NT$2.41.
4Q GM should improve sequentially: We expect 4Q revenue to decline slightly q/q
to NT$3,074mn (-3% q/q, +42% y/y), primarily due to the end of production for low-
end HTE copper foil and the lack of new capacity coming online. However, we expect
Co-Tech’s GM to expand 700bps q/q to 34%, driven by an improved product mix, a

~10% processing-fee increase effective from 4Q onward, and lower electricity rates
compared with 2Q–3Q, when summer electricity rates were in effect. We forecast
4Q EPS of NT$3.02 (+25% q/q, +102% y/y). For CY26, we estimate revenue will
increase approximately 47% y/y to NT$11.6bn, with GM expanding 760bps y/y to
29.3%, implying EPS of NT$9.62 (+129% y/y).
Potential processing-fee hike: Our supply-chain checks suggest that a key
competitor may announce processing-fee increases as early as December. If so, we

expect Co-Tech to follow with a price increase as well.
Upward revisions to our capacity assumptions ( Exhibit 3 ): Co-Tech has
announced plans to expand its greenfield capacity in Malaysia (report link). The
Malaysian expansion will be implemented in two phases, adding approximately 20k
tons of annual HVLP3/4 capacity from late 2028 onward. RG-series capacity is also
expected to increase modestly through the conversion of older HTE production
lines.

Raised PT to NT$745; stay OW: The increase in our price target is driven by our
earnings estimate changes for CY26-28. Our new price target implies 21x CY28 P/E
and the stock currently trades at only 12x CY28 P/E. We also find the valuation
attractive compared to  its past 3Y P/E average of ~20x.
What’s Changed

lower our 3Q GM forecast to factor in the higher production
costs and copper pricing. 4Q GM should improve meaningfully
sequentially. We raise our price target and stay OW.

Price target and valuation methodology
Our price target for Co-Tech increases  ~2% to NT$745: This implies 39x CY27 or 21x
CY28 P/E. Our price target is also our base case scenario value. It is derived from our

residual income (RI) model (similar to the rest of our tech hardware universe).
To calculate the fair value of the stock, we use the company's beginning equity plus the
present value of all expected residual income of earnings in excess of cost of capital. The
RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of capital and negative when it is below the
cost of capital. Our key assumptions include:
•  A cost of equity of 9.3% (beta of 1.0, equity risk premium of 8.7%, and risk-free rate

of 1.0%),
• a medium-term growth rate of 11.5% (lowered from 13% previously, as we enter a
higher base in CY27),
• a terminal growth rate of 3%.
The 21x CY28 P/E implied by our price target is above its  past 3Y average  of 20x. We deem
this valuation as fair, because Co-Tech is benefitting from an earnings growth cycle fueled
by stronger AI server/networking CCL demand, Co-Tech's continued mix improvement
(from HTE + RTF (RG) copper foils to advanced RTF (RG) + HVLP3/4 copper foils) and

continued  processing fee adjustments, which supports margin expansion. The stock is
trading at just 12x CY28 P/E, below Co-Tech's past 3Y average of 20x. Thus, we view the
risk reward as attractive.
Our bull- and bear-case values also increase ~2% to NT$1,225 and NT$305,
respectively.

Base case, derived from a residual income (RI) model. Key assumptions include a cost of

Consensus Price Target Distribution NT$570.00 NT$940.00
Source: Reﬁnitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

MS Rating
▪ Co-Tech has exited the low-margin HTE
copper foil business, which should support
proﬁtability improvement.
▪ Co-Tech has a dominant share in the
server R-PCB copper foil market with its RG
copper foils (equivalent to HVLP1/2), making
it a key beneﬁciary of global data center
infrastructure build-outs.
▪ Co-Tech is proactively expanding its
HVLP4 capacity to support key AI
customers' high-end copper foil demand. It
should have a 22% supply share by 2028, on

our estimates.
▪ Risk-reward is attractive with the stock
trading at 12x CY28 P/E vs the 3Y historical
average of 20x.

NT$1,225.00
35x 2028e P/E
We assume high-end servers and switches
see signiﬁcantly stronger growth in 2027-28,

driven by faster-than-expected new data
center build-outs and smooth ramp of new
generations of AI/networking chips; we also
assume copper foil demand from key
satellite customer will be stronger than
expected. We assume Co-Tech's copper foil
demand from the mentioned applications
remains robust while its product mix sees
continued improvement from the adoption
of HVLP4+ copper foil in more applications.
NT$745.00
21x 2028e P/E
Co-Tech continues to grow materially driven

by AI server demand growth, material grade
upgrades and better pricing power due to
scarce high-end capacity and continued shift
toward higher-end applications.
NT$305.00
9x 2028e P/E
We assume high-end servers and switches
see signiﬁcantly weaker demand in 2027-28,
driven by slowdown in new data center
build-outs and delayed ramp of new
generations of AI/networking chips; we also
assume copper foil demand from key
satellite customer will be stagnant. As a

result, we assume Co-Tech's copper foil
demand from the aforementioned
applications becomes sluggish and its
customers' migration to the HVLP4+
platform to be put on hold.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 3

NYPCB (8046.TW)
05 November 2026 Citi Research
3
NYPCB
Valuation
Our DCF-based TP for NYPCB is NT$1,700. With a risk-free rate of 5.00% (10-year government bond rate), a market risk
premium of 7% and an equity beta of 1.22 (YTD weekly share price), we calculate NYPCB's WACC as 13.4%. Our TP is equivalent
to 30x/13x 2027E EPS/BVPS, which reflects its high P/E multiple during upcycles. NYPCB traded at 15-20x P/E during its
2003-2008 upcycle driven by PC/NB demand strength. In 2010-11 when the market was recovering from the global financial

crisis, NYPCB traded at 25-35x P/E. In the last 2019-22 upcycle when ABF was in shortage, NYPCB traded at 30-40x P/E.
Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the NYPCB shares from reaching our target price include: 1) weaker-than-expected ABF
demand from key customers; 2) BT business seasonality weakness and fewer-than-expected price hikes; and 3) severe T glass
shortages that cause a bottleneck to its production.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

世芯-KY (3661 TT) 
營運顯著成長

永豐觀點 
2026~2027 年營收顯著成長。 
 投資評價與建議 
投資建議買進：隨主要客戶步入大規模量產階段，研究部預估世芯 2026 年稅後
EPS 122.49 元(原預估 140.8 元)，2027 年稅後 EPS 206.15 元(原預估 174.08 元)。
隨世芯 2Q~3Q26 啟動新一輪營運成長，2026~2027 年均顯著成長，維持買進建
議 ， 不過近期整體市場評價受調整 ， 研究部遂亦調整世芯本益比 ， 故目標價由5570
元調整至 4535 元(由 32X 改以 22X2027 年 EPS 評價)。

ESG 評析 
世芯企業永續評鑑整體為 B 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B 等與 B+等。

### Page 2

2

世芯為 IC 設計服務廠商：世芯於 2003 年成立，提供客戶 ASIC 與 SoC 的委託設
計服務，並協助客戶進行後端晶圓製造與封裝測試流程。世芯 3Q26 終端應用以
HPC 為重(佔比 87%)、消費電子 8%(比如數位相機、平板電腦與影像處理)、網路
通訊與利基型應用(包括娛樂機台、保全系統等)總計 5%。由於 HPC 採先進製程進
行，故世芯營收以製程別而言，高度集中於7nm 以下，7nm 以下營收佔比 89%。
世芯主攻北美市場，營收佔比 74%，其餘則分布於日本台灣等地。國內相關 IC 設
計服務公司包括創意與智原。 
3Q26 獲 利 優 於 預 期 ：世 芯公布 3Q26 營收 8.19 億美元 (+239%QoQ ，
+267%YoY)， 毛利率18%， 營業利益率13.9%， 稅後獲利1.02 億美元(+97%QoQ，
+131%YoY)， 稅後EPS 37.79 元 ， 獲利優於預期(研究部原預估稅後 EPS 34.68 元)，
主要是 AWS T3 挹注。世芯 3Q26 財報細項表現如下：(1) 美元營收季增 239%，
主要是 AWS T3 放量；(2) 毛利率由 2Q26 的 34.8%下滑至 18%，主要是量產比
重攀升，數個 NRE 專案遞延至 4Q26 所致。受限供應鏈緊俏(包括晶圓/載板/封裝
產能)，難免排擠承接其餘高毛利率產品的程度，使得 HPC 比重由前季的 50%飆
升至 87%；(3) 營業費用僅季增 4%， 反映營運槓桿效益顯現 。 整體而言 ， 世芯3Q26
財報顯示：隨 AWS T3 放量，公司(1) 營收 87%集中於 HPC。(2) 北美營收比重
由前季的 51%擴增至 84%，(3) 3nm/2nm 製程由前季的 47%攀升至 80%。 
4Q26 營收持續增溫：世芯表示(1) 4Q26 AWS T3 晶片挹注>3Q26，(2) AWS T4
進入 final implementation 階段 ， 預計4Q27 步入量產 。 整體而言 ， 公司預期4Q26
營收隨量產規模提升與 NRE 案件挹注而續增，毛利率與 3Q26 相仿(全年毛利率
>20%)，長期營業利益率 15%~19%(high teens) 。研究部預估世芯 4Q26 營收
344.72 億元 (+31%QoQ，+629%YoY)，毛利率 18%，稅後獲利 42.43 億元
(+30%QoQ，+186%YoY)，稅後 EPS 49.17 元。 
未來動能由於 AWS 下代晶片挹注、新增第二北美客戶：世芯營運自 2026 年重啟
成長態勢 ：(1) 隨今年 AWS T3 自五月底~六月初開始步入量產 ， 世芯2026~2027
年營收將主要由該案引領，公司預期該案 2027 年營收可望呈現三位數成長。世芯
認為示 T3 對公司 2027 年挹注更大 ， 主要是客戶給予公司更多晶圓配置 ；(2) AWS 
T4 維持預計 4Q27 量產，預計 2028 年進入大量生產階段，帶動 2028 年營運規
模向上提升。目前 T3 為 SoC 設計搭配 2.5D 封裝，而 T4 為 chiplet 架構與 GAA
製程，世芯負責個別晶片包括 compute die/IO die 等實體設計與最終封裝設計與
整合 ， 承擔腳色更為吃重 。(3) 第二個客戶為全新客戶(北美)預計 2Q27 開始量產 ，
可望成為第二大營收來源，公司期待該客戶為 10 億美元等級的業務。公司並期待
2027 年隨 AWS 與該位客戶挹注，2027 年整體營收可望呈現三位數成長。(4) 隨
AWS 今明年挹注、第二大客戶明年步入量產，不排除世芯2026 年營收 22 億~23

個股報告 
環球晶(6488) 
Novara 火災延後獲利回升，2027調價及新廠效益顯現 
投資評等 目標價 
逢低買進 $810 
前次投資建議：2026.08.05 
「逢低買進」 ，目標價: $1160

報告日期：2026.11.04 研 究 員：李彝安

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 26Q3 獲利/毛利不如預期。諾瓦拉廠火災延後營運回升時程，下修 2026 年、2027 年 EPS 
至  18.7 元、 27 元，價格調升與新產能自  27Q1 起反映，目標價下修至  810 元
(30x2027(F)PER)，維持「逢低買進」評等

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/03 收盤價： 685.0 ➢ 環球晶 26Q3 合併營收 148.7 億元（QoQ -2.3%／
YoY +2.6%）低於研究部原先預估，主營收季減主要受
7月義大利 Novara 8 吋廠火災影響，除部分出貨遞延
外，亦造成跨廠產能調配及產品組合變動，使毛利率承
壓單季 EPS 3.13 元，低於 26Q2 之 7.90 元，除火
災影響外，26Q2 認列之世創（Siltronic）評價利益本
季未再發生。 
➢ 需求面未受火災影響。排除爬坡中之新產線，既有 12 
吋產能滿載，8 吋與 6 吋接近滿載，26Q4 需求高於
原先預期，部分客戶為取得產能願將調價提前至 
26Q4。諾瓦拉後段製程仍在重建，支援廠區本身接近
滿載，26Q4 復原量有限。研究部下修 2026 年營收至 
596.4 億元、毛利率至 20.6%，2026 年 EPS 自 21.7 
元下修至 18.7 元。 
➢ 2027 年價格調升自 27Q1 起反映，晶圓庫存較高之客
戶對調價接受度較低，整體 ASP 調升將分散於各季反
映；新廠折舊於未來仍將持續增加。研究部就調價幅度
與新廠成本吸收速度採較保守假設，2027 年營收自 
836.2 億元下修至 762 億元、毛利率自 29.5%下修至 
26.6%，2027 年 EPS 自 34.1 元下修至 27 元。 
➢ 考量 2027 年調價幅度因客戶而異、新廠折舊仍在增
加，合併毛利率回升速度慢於前次預估；目標價自 
1,160 元下修至 810 元(30x2027(F)PER)，潛在上漲
空間 18.2%，評等維持「逢低買進」。

資料來源：CMoney；兆豐投顧預估

營 運 分 析 
全球前三大矽晶圓供應商，布局高階與第三代半導體材料 
環球晶圓股份有限公司（6488）成立於 2011 年，由中美矽晶半導體事業分
割成立，總部位於新竹科學園區，為全球前三大、台灣最大矽晶圓供應商，
亦為全球最大非日系矽晶圓廠。公司產品涵蓋  3 至 12 吋拋光、磊晶與  
Silicon-on-Insulator（SOI）晶圓，應用橫跨邏輯 IC、記憶體、功率元件、
車用、工控與射頻通訊等領域，主要客戶包括整合元件製造商（ Integrated 
Device Manufacturer, IDM） 與晶圓代工廠，競爭對手為信越化學、SUMCO、
Siltronic 與 SK Siltron。 
近年公司資本支出重心由既有亞洲據點延伸至美國與歐洲，推動 Texas 12 
吋矽晶圓新廠、Missouri SOI 擴產及義大利相關專案，以支應 AI、高效能
運算（High Performance Computing, HPC）、先進記憶體、矽光與在地化
供應鏈需求。美國商務部已核定對環球晶  Texas 與 Missouri 專案提供  
4.06 億美元補助，其中  Texas 廠將建立美國本土  300mm 矽晶圓量產能
力，Missouri 則聚焦 SOI 晶圓擴產。 
除傳統矽晶圓外，公司亦布局碳化矽（Silicon Carbide, SiC）、特殊晶圓與
方形矽晶圓等高附加價值材料，對應第三代半導體與先進封裝需求。

- 3 - 
個股報告

諾瓦拉廠火災壓低 26Q3 營收，營業利益仍較 26Q2 成長 
環球晶 26Q3 營收 148.71 億元（QoQ -2.3%／YoY +2.6%） ，低於研究部原
估 161.88 億元，主要受7月義大利 Novara 廠 8吋產線火災影響。該廠除供
應 8吋晶圓外，亦為集團 SOI產品提供基板，火災造成部分製程停工，其他
廠區須調整產能支援，使整體出貨受到影響。公司表示，若未發生火災，26Q3
營收原有機會季增逾 12%，顯示營收下滑主要來自供給受限，而非客戶需求
轉弱。 
26Q3 毛利率 20.0%（QoQ -0.6ppt／YoY +1.6ppt） ，主要受新產能爬坡成本、
折舊增加及跨廠調配造成產品組合變動影響。營業利益 14.54 億元（QoQ 
+2.2%） ，營益率9.8%（QoQ +0.4ppt） ，部分受惠Siltronic股價變動使相關
酬勞提列減少 。 稅後淨利15.05 億元 （QoQ -60.2%／YoY -23.6%），EPS 3.13
元，較26Q2 的 7.90 元明顯下滑，主因Siltronic股價於 26Q2 上漲約 56%，
公司認列較高評價利益，26Q3 股價變動不大，業外貢獻因而減少。

Novara後段製程仍待修復，26Q4營收恢復季增 
Novara 8 吋廠主要製程已陸續恢復，磊晶產品亦開始出貨，惟部分後段製程
仍在重建，暫由集團其他廠區支援。由於支援廠區稼動率已高，且各廠產品
規格及客戶認證要求不同，可轉移產能有限，短期出貨仍難恢復至火災前水
準。部分關鍵設備交期較長，後段製程完全復原時程尚未確定；相關財產損
失及營業中斷保險理賠已啟動，惟金額尚未確認，公司尚未認列理賠收益。
至於 Novara 12 吋新廠位於獨立廠房，未受火災影響，目前已開始量產出貨，
擴產進度持續推進。 
26Q4 客戶需求優於原先預期，部分客戶為確保產能供應，願提前接受新價
格，惟多數訂單仍適用原有長約或2025 年議定價格，加上Novara 產能復原
有限 ， 預期26Q4 營收將優於 26Q3， 但季增幅度低於12%。 研究部預估26Q4
營收 155.78 億元（ QoQ +4.8% ／YoY +7.4% ） ，毛利率21.0%（QoQ 
+1.0ppt） ，稅後淨利17.54 億元，EPS 3.67元。由於 26Q4 實際調價幅度有
限，短期營收改善仍以既有產能出貨及 Novara 逐步復原為主。

既有產能接近滿載，2027年新長約帶動 ASP改善 
排除尚在爬坡的新產線 ， 環球晶既有12吋產能已滿載 ，8吋及 6吋接近滿載，
部分小尺寸晶圓需求亦超過現有供給。隨半導體廠陸續擴充先進製程與記憶
體產能，晶圓投片需求有望增加，惟新產能仍須經過設備安裝及客戶認證，
具備量產資格的 12吋晶圓供給短期難以快速增加。公司預期2027 年矽晶圓
供需將較 2026 年緊俏，為新一輪價格調整提供支撐。 
價格方面，能源、原物料、人力及運輸成本上升，加上新廠建置成本提高，
環球晶正與客戶協商新長約價格。由於既有長約仍須履行，且不同客戶庫存
及需求狀況不一，調價將依產品規格、供需及換約時點逐案進行，並非採取

評等分級：買進（預期未來六個月股價漲幅超過+30%）; 逢低買進（預期未來六個月股價漲幅介於+15%與+30%之間）; 區間操
作（預期未來六個月股價漲跌幅介於-15%與+15%之間）; 逢高賣出（預期未來六個月股價跌幅介於-15%與-30%之間）；賣出（預

- 4 - 
個股報告 
固定漲幅。部分庫存較高的客戶對價格調整接受度較低，公司會優先將產能
分配予願意接受新價格、簽訂長約或支付預付款的客戶。研究部預期隨2027
年新長約陸續生效、部分現貨報價改善及高階產品占比提升，整體ASP將逐
季回升。 
12吋 SOI需求超過既有擴產規劃，矽光子提供後續成長動能 
環球晶 12吋 SOI 需求受惠製程由 8吋轉向 12 吋，以及矽光子、RF-SOI及
Power SOI應用成長，客戶需求已超過密蘇里廠既有擴產規劃。公司目前優
先透過設備認證及製程去瓶頸提高現有產能，部分 RF-SOI 及矽光子產品已
進入量產。由於密蘇里廠現有廠房及基礎設施已就位，下一階段擴產主要以
增購設備為主，資本效率可望優於第一階段。 
公司正與多家客戶洽談長期供貨協議及預付款安排，規劃中的客戶承諾可支
持擬新增 SOI產能逾九成需求，惟相關合約及擴產決策仍未完全確定。後續
投資時程將取決於現有產能獲利改善程度、客戶長期承諾及政府補助條件，
研究部暫不將尚未核定的新增產能全數納入 2027 年營收預估。此外，SiC
需求逐步由電動車延伸至資料中心、電網及工業應用，12吋 SiC仍處初期爬
坡階段，預期 2027 年營收貢獻仍低，對短期獲利影響有限。

營業利益 60.34 億元，稅後淨利 89.30 億元（YoY +22.1%），EPS 18.68
元；2027 年營收 762.03 億元（YoY +27.8%），毛利率 26.6%，營業利益
135.76 億元（YoY +125.0%），稅後淨利 129.04億元（YoY +44.5%），
EPS 26.99元。

- 5 - 
個股報告 
本次下修 2026 年 EPS 由 21.69 元至 18.68 元，主要反映 26Q3 Novara 火
災影響大於原先預期，以及26Q4 出貨恢復與毛利率改善幅度有限；2027年
EPS 則由 34.12 元下修至 26.99 元，主要反映營收由 836 億元降至 762 億
元，毛利率由 29.5%下修至 26.6%。考量 2027 年新長約價格逐步調整、
Novara 產能復原，以及美國、義大利與日本新廠出貨增加，研究部預期本業
獲利仍將逐季回升，惟新產能折舊與價格協商進度將拖累獲利改善幅度。

從結論來看，環球晶 26Q3 受 Novara 火災影響，延後原先預期的營收與毛
利率回升時程，不過既有產線稼動率仍維持高檔，2027 年調價及海外新廠出
貨增加的成長方向未變。考量 2027 年矽晶圓價格調整幅度及新廠稼動率提
升速度仍具不確定性，研究部將評價倍數由34 倍下修至 30倍 2027F PER。
以 2027 年預估 EPS 26.99 元計算，目標價由 1,160 元調降至 810 元，潛在
上漲空間 18.2%，維持「逢低買進」評等。

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

(005930) 
삼성전자 
2026.09.29

반도체 손인준 
6154 / sij@eugenefn.com

긴 호흡으로 
 투자의견 STRONG BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 560,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 270,000원(9/28)

 삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익이 103.6조원(+16%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 메모리 가격 등 전반적인 반도체 업황에 대한 기존 가정은 대체로 
유지했으나, 최근 원화 강세에 따라 원/달러 환율 가정을 1,440원에서 
1,400원으로 낮추고 MX 사업부의 적자 확대를 반영해 실적 추정치를 하
향 조정했다. 또한 2Q26 재고가 3Q26 매출로 인식되는 과정에서 발생하
는 추가 성과급 충당금 부담도 반영했다.  
 DS 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 104.0조원(+17%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 서버 DRAM과 eSSD 중심의 가격 인상이 이어지며, DRAM과 
NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +17%, +18%QoQ 상승할 것으로 추정한
다. 1cnm 수율 개선과 HBM4의 빠른 램프업도 실적 개선에 기여할 전망이
다. Foundry/LSI는 HBM4 베이스다이 생산 확대, Groq 3 LPU 양산 및 선
단 공정 가격 인상에 힘입어 하반기 매출 성장이 예상된다. 다만 성과급 충
당금과 감가상각비 부담으로 영업적자 축소 폭은 제한적일 것으로 보인다. 
 DX 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 -1.8조원(적자지속qoq)을 기록할 것으로 추
정한다. 삼성전자는 메모리 가격 급등에도 제품 가격 인상을 제한하며 시장 
점유율 확대에 주력하고 있다. 이러한 가격 정책과 갤럭시 Z 폴드8 등 신제
품 흥행에 힘입어 판매량은 당초 예상치를 상회할 것으로 보인다. 다만 원
가 상승분의 전가가 제한되면서 영업적자 폭은 크게 확대될 전망이다.  
 삼성전자의 4Q26 영업이익은 114.9조원(+11%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. DRAM과 NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +11%, +12%QoQ 상승
하며 견조한 흐름을 이어갈 것으로 보인다. 환율 가정 조정에 따라 2026년
과 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 각각 365.2조원(+735%yoy), 595.8조원
(+63%YoY)으로 낮췄다. 4Q26~1Q27 범용 DRAM·NAND 및 2027년 
HBM 가격 협상 환경은 공급업체에 유리하게 전개되고 있어, 향후 가격 가
정의 추가 상향 가능성이 높다고 판단한다.  
 극심한 DRAM 공급 부족으로 인한 HBM 적층 단수 하향 조정(base die 수
요 증가), Memory Wall에 대응하기 위한 3D Stacked DRAM·zHBM 도입 
논의 등은 파운드리의 기술적 가치를 부각시키는 요인이다. 파운드리의 수
익성 회복 속도는 완만할 전망이지만, 메모리와 파운드리를 연계한 턴키 비
즈니스의 시너지는 AI 반도체 시장 내 경쟁력을 높이고, 메모리 시장 점유
율 확대에도 기여할 것으로 보인다. 적극적인 메모리 가격 인상 정책으로 
업계 최고 수준의 Blended ASP도 장기간 유지할 것으로 예상한다. 
 이제 메모리 업종 주가는 업황의 지속 가능성을 확인하고 꾸준한 주주환원
을 반영하며, 중장기적으로 점진적인 상승세를 이어갈 것으로 판단한다. 삼
성전자는 메모리·파운드리를 아우르는 턴키 역량과 가격 정책에 기반한 실
적 차별화를 통해 피어 그룹 내 경쟁력을 높여갈 것으로 보인다.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.

The government is curbing inflation
◆ Policy factors are pushing down the inflation rate
In June, core inflation (all items, less fresh food) was 1.6%, staying under 2%
for the fifth consecutive month. Food price inflation has slowed, and a major

reason for this is a fallback in rice prices, which soared last year. Gasoline
subsidies are also continuing to curtail energy price increases.
Inflation looks set to hover in the 2% range in the second half of 2026. Although
energy subsidies and other government measures will soften inflationary pressure
to some extent, with imported inflationary pressure building, prices are expected
to be pushed up as higher costs are passed through to selling prices. From

FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items will
serve to keep prices down, and inflation is expected to drop into the 1% range.
◆ The Bank of Japan (BOJ) will keep its policy rate unchanged
At its July monetary policy meeting, the BOJ decided to keep its policy rate
unchanged.
Long-term interest rates remain high. This reflects concerns about inflation
resulting from the increase in oil prices and expectations of an expansion in fiscal

expenditure.
The BOJ is expected to step up the pace of interest rate hikes while closely
monitoring the impact of higher rates on the real economy and financial/capital
markets. The next rate hike is expected to be in September.
Long-term interest rates look likely to rise gradually, reflecting heightened
anticipation of rate hikes by the BOJ and increased fiscal spending by the
government. If jitters about a deterioration in fiscal discipline resulting from such

measures as energy subsidies or the consumption tax cut take hold, there is also
a risk that long-term interest rates will rise.

◆ Surging crude oil prices have become a source of upward pressure on
prices
With crude oil prices having spiked against the backdrop of the worsening situation
in the Middle East, corporate input costs have risen sharply, and pressure for price
pass-through is intensifying. Looking at the Final Demand-Intermediate Demand
price indexes, the margin of price increases at the upstream stage far exceeds that at
the downstream stage, and price pass-through pressure is also substantial at the

downstream stage.
According to estimates based on past data, the impact of rising oil prices involves
a time lag. Specifically, the push-up effect on the consumer price inflation rate
increases for around 10 months and continues to exert influence for a long period
thereafter.
◆ Price pass-through willingness is also stronger than before
Notably, food product manufacturers are planning to raise prices for a huge
number of items during the summer and beyond. According to Teikoku Databank, the

number of items slated for price increases as of the end of July, when it conducted its
latest survey, was higher than in the May and June surveys.
If government subsidies for electricity/gas bills and gasoline prices are scaled back
or ended, both energy and food prices will increase concurrently, and the consumer
price inflation rate may rise again.
Attention also needs to be paid to the possibility that price pass-through driven by
elevated oil prices will become prolonged. In the 2020s, the rate of price increases in

transactions between companies has been higher than in the 2010s, and firms'
medium-term outlooks for selling prices have also shifted upward. Events that trigger
cost increases, such as heightened geopolitical tensions, have been occurring
repeatedly, and companies may be more willing to pass through higher costs to
prices than they were before.

### Page 10

The economy is expected to continue to recover, but downside risks are present
◆ The economy is projected to stay on the recovery track
The Japanese economy should continue its gradual recovery. WTI crude oil
futures prices are expected to fall moderately going forward.
In the corporate sector, government support for investment is providing a tailwind,
and demand for investment for labor saving and digital transformation remains
robust. In addition, rising global demand for AI-related goods should support exports.

Supply chain disruptions resulting from the deterioration in the Middle East situation
will begin to ease, and corporate production activity is expected to gradually recover.
In the household sector, inflationary pressure is expected to strengthen from the
summer onward due to higher crude oil prices. In response, the government will
probably ease the inflationary pressure through measures such as energy subsidies.
While the pace of increase in personal consumption is likely to slow somewhat,

consumption is expected to remain resilient amid continued high wage growth. From
FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items should also
support personal consumption.
◆ The Middle East situation and rising interest rates constitute risks
Real GDP growth is projected to remain solid, registering +0.8% in FY2026 and
+0.9% in FY2027.
However, if the situation in the Middle East worsens again or becomes prolonged,
there is a risk that downward pressure on the economy will intensify, mainly through

three channels: 1) weaker exports, 2) raw material supply constraints, and 3) rising
prices. In addition, if market concerns about the fiscal stance of the Takaichi
government grow, the yen could weaken further, and long-term interest rates could
rise, potentially forcing the scaling-back or scrapping of expansionary policies such
as investment support measures, energy subsidies, and lower consumption tax.
Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026

The Japan Research Institute, Limited- ８ -

NZ ドル相場の行方を左右する総選挙と RBNZ の政策運営 
～インフレ圧力は根強い一方、政権交代に伴う政策枠組みの変更が不透明要因に～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ニュージーランドでは、インフレ高進を背景に RBNZ が９月に２会合連続の利上げを実施し
たものの、11 月７日の総選挙を巡る不透明感がＮＺドル相場の重しとなってきた。 新興政党
のオポチュニティ党が連立協議のカギを握る可能性が高まっているうえ、仮に左派・労働党
を中心とする政権への交代が実現すれば、RBNZ の政策目標に雇用の最大化が再び加えられる
と見込まれる。そのため、追加利上げのハードルが高まる可能性が意識されている。 
○ ７-９月のインフレ率は前年比＋3.7％と鈍化し、市場予想も下回ったものの、引き続き目標
レンジを上回っている。エネルギーや食料品を中心にインフレ圧力が後退する一方、サービ
ス物価には上昇圧力が広がる兆しがうかがえる。さらに、原油価格の高止まりや干ばつによ
る食料インフレの懸念も残る。金融市場では早期利上げ観測がくすぶるものの、今回の物価
統計を受けて見方が修正される可能性もあり、先行きのＮＺドル相場は総選挙の結果と、そ
の後のRBNZ の政策枠組みに関する議論に左右される展開が予想される。

【RBNZは追加利上げ実施も、総選挙を巡る不透明感がNZドル相場の重しとなる展開が続いた】 
ニュージーランドでは、RBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）が９月の定例理事会において２会合
連続の利上げを決定した （注１） 。 背景には、2025 年以降のインフレ率が加速してRBNZ の定める目
標レンジ（１～３％）の上限を上回る伸びで推移していることがある。さらに、イラン情勢の悪化
による原油価格の上昇がエネルギーインフレを招き、４-６月のインフレ率は前年比＋4.1％と2023
年 10-12 月以来の高い伸びとなった。こうした事情は、RBNZ の断続的な利上げ実施を後押ししたと
考えられる。 
一方、通貨ＮＺドル相場は上値の重い展開をみせてきた。同国では、11 月７日に議会（代議院：
一院制 （総議席数120） ） の総選挙の実施が予定されており、選挙結果を巡る不透明感が影響してい
るとみられる。世論調査によれば、右派の国民党、ないし左派の労働党を中心とする連立政権がい
ずれも多数派を形成できず、新興政党・オポチュニティ党がキャスティングボートを握る可能性が

注１ ９月２日付レポート「RBNZ は緩やかな引き締め示唆､NZ ドル相場の行方はどうなる？」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/654752.html）

2/3 
浮上している（注２） 。 同党は、都市部で年間1.75％、 農村部で0.5％の土地税を新設し、 それを財
源の一部に原則18 歳以上の国民に年間1.94 万ＮＺドルのベーシックインカム （ＢＩ） に相当する
「市民所得」を支給する税制改正を公約に掲げる。国民党、労働党ともに同党が掲げる税制改正案
の採用を否定しており、実現のハードルは高い。しかし、それ以外の政策の方向性については、左
派陣営と比較的親和性が高いとみられるため、選挙後に行われる連立協議の行方が注目される。 
こうしたなか、政治的な争点のひとつにRBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）の政策運営が浮上し
ていることも注目される。労働党を中心
とする左派陣営 は、RBNZ のマンデート
（政策目標）の変更を公約に掲げてお
り、仮に政権交代が実現すれば、持続可
能な雇用の最大化が再び加えられると
見込まれる。足元の雇用環境は、直近４
-６月の失業率が10 年ぶりの高水準とな
ったことが確認されている（図１）。この
ため、RBNZ のブレマン総裁は９月会合後
の記者会見において、選挙結果は政策決
定に影響しないとの見方を示したもの
の、追加利上げのハードルが高まることは避けられない。こうした事情も、ＮＺドル相場の重しに
なってきたと考えられる。

【金融市場はRBNZの早期利上げを意識するも、NZドル相場の行方は総選挙の結果がカギを握る】 
このため、金融市場においては、RBNZ
の政策運営を占ううえで 物価動向に注
目が集まった。７-９月の消費者物価は
前年比＋ 3.7％に伸びが鈍化したもの
の、 ５四半期連続で目標レンジを上回る
推移となった（図２）。ただし、 この水準
は RBNZ が９月会合に際して示した見通
し（前年比＋3.9％）を下回るとともに、
事前の市場予想をも下回る伸びにとど
まった。前期比も＋0.66％と前期 （同＋
1.49％）から上昇ペースが鈍化してお
り、 原油価格の上昇一服を受けてガソリンなどエネルギー価格が下落したほか、 生鮮品をはじめと

3/3 
する食料品価格の上昇ペースが鈍化しており、 生活必需品を中心にインフレ圧力は後退した。一方
で、食料品やエネルギーを除いたコアインフレ率は前年比＋2.9％と前期（同＋2.6％）から伸びが
加速したものの、 引き続き目標レンジ内で推移している。 とはいえ、 前期比は＋1.1％と前期 （同＋
0.4％） から上昇ペースが加速しており、 財価格は比較的落ち着いた動きをみせる一方、 サービス物
価で幅広く上昇圧力が強まる動きが確認されるなど、 インフレ圧力が徐々に広がりをみせる兆しも
うかがえる。 
足元では、 イラン情勢を巡る不透明感を理由に原油価格は高止まりしており、 先行きもエネルギ
ーインフレが長引く可能性が高まっている。さらに、エルニーニョ現象の発生を受けて、同国では
東部や北部を中心に、乾燥・干ばつによる農業生産への悪影響が懸念されており、食料インフレや
景気の下押しが懸念される状況にある。 
総選挙においてはインフレも主要な争
点となっており、早期のインフレ抑制に
向けて、RBNZ が前倒しで利上げに動くと
の見方も金融市場に出ており、このとこ
ろの ＮＺドル相場が持ち直 す一因とな
ってきた可能性がある （図３） 。今回の物
価統計が市場予想を下回ったこと を受
けて、 早期利上げ観測が修正される可能
性もある。先行きのＮＺドル相場の行方
を巡っては、総選挙の結果と、その後の
RBNZ の政策枠組みに関する議論に左右
される可能性にも注意が必要と考えられる。

Document 473: US Daily_ Why Has the US Economy Been So Resilient to Higher Rates and Higher Oil Prices_

### Page 1

Abhay Duggirala 
+1(202)585-6760 |

US Daily: Why Has the US Economy Been So Resilient to Higher Rates 
and Higher Oil Prices?
28 September 2026 | 10:38AM EDT
We estimate that the economy grew at a 3.4% pace in Q3 and forecast 2.2% n
growth in 2026 overall on a Q4/Q4 basis, close to our estimate of potential GDP 
growth and only 0.3pp below our start-of-the-year forecast before the large 
increases in interest rates and oil prices. Why, we are often asked, has the US 
economy proven so resilient to these shocks? 
While interest rates have risen substantially this year, our financial conditions n
index framework implies that the economic impact should be nearly offset by the 
effects of higher equity prices, narrower credit spreads, and a weaker dollar, 
resulting in only a 0.1pp net hit to 2026 GDP growth. While the net impact of this 
year’s market moves on overall GDP is modest, the effects on some parts of the 
economy—especially the hit to housing—are more meaningful. 
We estimate that the increase in oil and refined product prices this year, together n
with the path our commodities strategists expect through year-end, will subtract 
0.3pp from 2026 growth. The drag comes from consumption as higher energy 
prices erode real purchasing power. While US energy producers were reluctant to 
boost investment when oil prices first rose, the increase in the rig count index in 
recent months points to some offsetting boost to GDP in Q3 and Q4. 
Our analysis suggests that slightly tighter financial conditions and higher oil n
prices should together be roughly a 0.4pp drag on GDP growth. The downgrade 
we have made to our forecast for the year, in part based on incoming data, is only 
slightly smaller than that, with the slight outperformance coming largely from 
consumer spending. This suggests that the economy’s resilience is less puzzling 
than it might appear at first.

### Page 2

Why Has the US Economy Been So Resilient to Higher Rates and Higher Oil Prices?

We now estimate that the economy grew at a 3.4% pace in Q3 and forecast 2.2% growth 
in 2026 overall on a Q4/Q4 basis, close to our 2.3% estimate of potential GDP growth 
and only 0.3pp below our start-of-the-year forecast when we did not expect large 
increases in interest rates and oil prices. In today’s Daily, we ask why the US economy has 
proven so resilient to these surprises. 
Part of the answer is that financial conditions have changed little on net since January, as 
the substantial rise in interest rates has been nearly offset by the effects of higher equity 
prices, narrower credit spreads, and a weaker dollar (Exhibit 1, left). Our estimated GDP 
growth impulse from the FCI is therefore little changed from the start of the year (Exhibit 
1, right), implying only a 0.1pp hit to 2026 Q4/Q4 GDP growth.

While the net impact of this year’s market moves on overall GDP is modest, the effects 
on some components of GDP are more meaningful. We use the Fed’s macroeconomic 
model FRB/US to estimate how the particular combination of market moves this year 
should affect each component of GDP . We find a meaningful hit from higher rates to 
residential investment, offset by slight boosts to consumption and business investment 
(Exhibit 2).

Start of the Year, as Higher Interest Rates Have Been Offset by Tighter Credit Spreads, Higher Equity Prices, and a Weaker 
Dollar

### Page 3

What about the effect of higher oil and refined products prices? Our rule of thumb 
implies that a sustained $10/barrel increase in oil prices lowers GDP growth by just over 
0.1pp through the consumption channel as higher energy costs erode real purchasing 
power. Applied to the path oil prices have taken this year, the unusual widening in 
refined product spreads, and our commodities strategists’ forecasts for prices through 
year-end, the rule of thumb implies a 0.4pp drag on GDP growth. 
While US oil producers were initially reluctant to respond to higher prices by increasing 
capex because of the uncertainty of the war with Iran and the outlook for oil prices, the 
increase in the rig count index in Q3 (Exhibit 3) points to a pickup in energy capex more 
recently. We estimate that the consumption drag and capex boost will net out to a 0.3pp 
hit to GDP growth from the oil shock. Two alternative approaches, FRB/US simulations 
and a model estimated using exogenous oil shocks, deliver similar estimates on average.1 
1 We use the series of exogenous oil shocks from Känzig (2021), “The Macroeconomic Effects of Oil Supply 
News: Evidence from OPEC Announcements” to estimate the effects of the current oil shock on GDP growth.

Overall GDP , but Will Impose a Larger Drag on Residential Investment, Which Is More 
Sensitive to Higher Interest Rates

component under this year's changes in market conditions versus a counterfactual where
conditions were unchanged from the start of the year.

### Page 4

Our analysis suggests that slightly tighter financial conditions and higher oil prices 
should together be roughly a 0.4pp drag on GDP growth. The downgrade we have made 
to our forecast for the year, in part based on incoming data, is only slightly smaller than 
that, suggesting that the puzzle of economic resilience is smaller than it might appear at 
first. 
The economy’s slight outperformance relative to what we might have expected largely 
comes from consumer spending. At the start of the year, we forecasted consumption 
growth of 2.4% in 2026 Q4/Q4. Under the market and commodity price moves that 
actually materialized, our oil shock model implies a 0.6pp hit to that forecast, partially 
offset by a 0.2pp boost from higher equity prices in our wealth effects model, netting to 
an expected 2.0% growth in consumption this year. Against that, we now estimate—in 
part based on the surprisingly solid incoming data in recent months—that consumption 
will grow 2.2%, a modest upside surprise of 0.2pp.

Document 487: What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22)

WHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM
THE MEASURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO
TURN THE SCREW?
Marianne Mueller and Pascal Devaux
issue 19
21 October 2026
The 2022 energy crisis clearly highlighted the issue of electricity prices in Europe — and, more broadly, the issue of energy prices. As
a result, European manufacturers’ competitiveness has been severely undermined when compared with the United States and China.

By reigniting tensions over electricity prices, the conflict that began in the Middle East in late February 2026 further underscores the
continent’s vulnerability to fossil fuel imports, despite the expansion of renewable energy. Faced with an environment of potential sharp
and sudden price rises, European governments do not have the same room for manoeuvre.
While the additional costs borne by European manufacturers are mainly linked to the share of gas in national electricity mixes, we will

first illustrate that, in Germany, grid operation and maintenance costs, as well as taxation, are also contributing significantly to these
costs.
We will then explore the solutions proposed by the German government under the new European measures regulating state aid to
electricity-intensive industries.
Finally, our estimates will show that, while these measures make Germany more competitive against some European countries, they
do not place it on an equal competitive footing with the United States or China. Nevertheless, Germany has made significant progress

in decarbonising and making its electricity mix more flexible from a European perspective. This should help to ensure that electricity costs
are reduced in the medium term.

### Page 2

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 19 / 21 October 2026

for a changing
worldWHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM THE MEA- SURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO TURN THE SCREW?
GAS: A KEY FACTOR IN THE ADDITIONAL ELECTRICITY COSTS
FACED BY EUROPEAN INDUSTRY
European manufacturers are still paying significantly higher elec -
tricity costs than their American and Chinese competitors. In 2019,

there was a 30% difference between the prices charged to European
electricity-intensive industries (corresponding to the lower end of the
industrial price range) and the average US industrial price. In 2025,
it stood at nearly 100% according to the IEA (2026) 1, after peaking at
an average of 148% between 2022 and 2023. There is currently a 50%
difference with their Chinese competitors2.
The disruption to Russian gas supplies since 2022 is behind the rising

gas prices, particularly as the EU has had to turn to liquefied natural
gas3 (LNG), which is more expensive than gas transported via pipe -
line and, above all, exposes Europe to global gas market conditions.
The growth of LNG has, in fact, intensified competition between the
major consumption regions, primarily Europe and Asia. European and
Asian prices now tend to move more in tandem (Chart 1). However, in
China, the prevalence of coal — a widely available local resource —

and a more regulated electricity price help to keep industrial prices
lower. At the same time, the United States enjoys plentiful domestic
gas, which is produced at a low cost, driven by the sharp rise in shale
oil and gas production over the past decade. By contrast, since 2022,
Europe has been more exposed to the cost of imported gas (90% of its
total consumption, according to Eurostat), while the United States and
China have enjoyed plentiful domestic resources.

Fluctuations and the level of gas prices are passed on to Eu -
ropean electricity prices via the ‘merit order’ marginal pricing
mechanism, with the wholesale price determined by the margi -
nal cost of the last (and most expensive) electricity source called
upon to meet demand.
As a result, gas plays a decisive role, with gas-fired power sta -
tions setting the price in around 40% of cases in Europe4, with si-
gnificant variations depending on national electricity mixes. It is

close to this average in Germany, below it in Spain (26%) and
France (17%), and above it in Italy (73%) 5 in 2025 6 7. Therefore,
electricity prices are still sensitive to fluctuations in gas prices,
even when electricity is not generated from gas (Chart 2). This
pass-through is particularly pronounced when demand rises
and requires the use of more expensive sources, or when supply
constraints arise (for example, during extreme heatwaves that
reduce wind-power generation or disrupt the cooling of nuclear

power plants).
1 Prices – Electricity 2026 – Analysis – IEA
2 The difference may be even larger for other manufacturers who do not receive the same support as electricity-intensive sectors.
3 Imports of Russian gas (via pipeline and LNG) accounted for around 45% of total European imports in 2021, compared with 14% in Q2 2026 (Bruegel, natural gas imports).
4 ACER, 2026, Key developments in EU electricity and gas markets, 2026 Monitoring report.
5 In Italy, in response to sharply rising gas prices in 2026, the government is currently negotiating a potential option of capping the prices of gas supplied to power stations in

order to limit the rise in the wholesale price of electricity. However, this can only be a temporary general mechanism, with the potentially major cost being borne by public funds
and/or households.
6 Eurelectric, 2026, Power Barometer.
7 EMBER, 2026, Latest energy shock reminds Europe of its risky reliance on gas.
8 Energy shock: 2026 vs. 2022 dashboard – 2nd October 2026 – Economic Studies – BNP Paribas
However, for the time being, the 2026 energy crisis is having a far

smaller effect on electricity prices than in 2022. On the one hand, the
2026 peak reached by the European TTF benchmark is still well below
that recorded in 20228.
In addition, there has been significant progress in decarbonising Eu -
ropean electricity mixes since 2022, which has helped to mitigate, on
average, the pass-through of gas prices to electricity prices.

SOME EUROPEAN COUNTRIES HAVE MANAGED TO STAY
COMPETITIVE, BUT GERMANY IS STILL THE EXCEPTION
Electricity prices partly vary due to countries’ electricity mixes
(Chart 3). Electricity-intensive manufacturers in countries where the
share of fossil energy is high face higher costs (Poland, Italy and Ire -
land) (Chart 4). Conversely, countries with a less carbon-intensive mix

(particularly France and Spain in recent times) are less directly ex -
posed to these fluctuations, with costs closer to the United States and
China (Chart 5).
The situation in Germany is paradoxical, as, despite progressively
greening its electricity mix and wholesale electricity prices (averaging
90 EUR/MWh over the 2023–2025 period) and average electricity costs
(across all industrial sectors) in line with the European average, the most
energy-intensive industries face higher costs than the European average

(and even more so compared with international competitors). This is
mainly due to grid costs and a tax burden above EU standards (Chart 6).
9 Eurostat data are used to compare prices with the rest of the EU. However, given that electricity prices are not uniform for businesses at a European level, there may be differences
between Eurostat and national sources. Jäger P ., 2025, “Cautionary tale or model for Europe? How member states should react to Germany’s new electricity subsidy”, Hertie School,

Jacques Delors Centre.
10 P . Devaux, 2026, “European Union: low carbon transition and energy sovereignty, a path fraught with obstacles”
In the first half of 2026, for the most electricity-intensive industries,
the difference compared with the European average was 9%, based on
the prices excluding taxes and levies provided by Eurostat. This gap is
mainly due to the cost of accessing the grid. If all taxes and levies are
included, the difference rises to 21% (Chart 7)9.

EUROPE’S APPROACH OF ADOPTING BOTH STRUCTURAL
PROGRAMMES AND TARGETED SUPPORT MEASURES
European programmes to support the low-carbon transition pursue
objectives relating to climate action (reducing greenhouse gas emis -
sions), sovereignty10 (increasing energy self-sufficiency) and competi -
tiveness (reducing the volatility and costs associated with fossil fuels).
The RePowerEU programme, which was launched in 2022 in response
to the energy crisis, primarily aims to reduce imports of Russian gas

and, more generally, to accelerate the low-carbon transition. Although
Europe has reduced its dependence on imports of Russian fossil fuels,
there has still not been enough progress around the energy transition
and cross-border connectivity. This is due to governance constraints

and struggles in implementing the programme in practice within each
Member State11.
State aid is still the main vehicle for this. In response to the 2021–
2022 energy crisis, the European Union temporarily relaxed the rules
on this type of aid in order to support businesses facing rising energy
prices. Any intervention of this kind, provided that it is selective and

could affect trade within the single market, must be authorised in ad -
vance by the European Commission in principle. Since June 2025, the
Clean Industrial Deal State Aid Framework (CISAF) has provided a more
sustainable framework for these kinds of interventions. In particular,
this framework, which runs until the end of 2030, authorises Member
States to grant temporary aid to electricity-intensive businesses to off-
set the cost of electricity. The aid must be targeted and come with

decarbonisation commitments12. Against the backdrop of the crisis in
the Middle East, the temporary METSAF scheme, adopted in April 2026,
helped (in relation to measures falling under the CISAF) to increase the
proportion of additional costs offset by governments from 50% to 70%,
thereby offering additional support to the most at-risk sectors.
GERMANY IS STILL SUFFERING FROM INSUFFICIENT IN-
VESTMENT IN ITS ELECTRICITY NETWORKS, WHICH THE
AID SCHEMES ARE ATTEMPTING TO OFFSET

The issue of electricity costs is a particularly hot issue in Germany,
given the importance of industry to the economy (around 20% of va -
lue added, one of the highest levels in Europe) and its exposure to
international competition. Furthermore, the delay in modernising
electricity infrastructure (only 22.6% of the high-voltage lines planned
for 2025 had actually been completed by that date) is causing bottle -
necks between the production areas in the north and the consump -

11 European Court of Auditors, 2026, “Implementation of the REPowerEU plan must be stepped up”.
12 It applies to the most electricity-intensive activities, which are seeing their competitiveness threatened by international competition, and follows the 4x50 principle: 50% of
electricity consumption is eligible for a subsidy; Of the eligible proportion, the subsidy may not exceed 50% of the wholesale price;
The final wholesale price paid by the company must not be less than €50/MWh;

50% of the aid received must be reinvested in decarbonisation (renewable energy generation, storage and improvements in energy efficiency); Around 90 sectors are covered by this
reform, accounting for approximately 2,000 companies, including in the chemical, steel and paper industries. The scheme, which is strictly regulated, will run for just three years
(from 1st January 2026 to the end of 2028) at an estimated total cost of between EUR 3 and 5 bn, financed by the special Climate and Transformation Fund.

13 Bundesnetzagentur Monitoring Report (2015) and Bundesnetzagentur Monitoring Report (2025). These costs mainly cover the costs of physical infrastructure; operational,
regulatory and compliance costs; and costs associated with using the transmission and distribution network.
14 The Industriestrompreis (industrial electricity price) is the public subsidy mechanism aiming to reduce the price of electricity for some industries.
15 The indirect carbon cost compensation mechanism is a government support measure for electricity-intensive industries, which aims to offset the cost of carbon-emission

allowances for energy producers.
tion centres in the south. As a result, the costs of accessing services
have increased fivefold over 10 years (rising from EUR 1 bn in 2014
to EUR 5.2 bn in 2024 13). At the same time, as we have already seen,
taxation and grid charges are hitting industry hard, with levies on large
consumers far exceeding the European average despite some support
mechanisms being put in place for the most electricity-intensive com-

panies (such as the Bandlastprivileg measure to reduce grid tariffs).
As a result, German industrial production has suffered in particular
due to rising energy prices in recent years, including electricity, with
electricity-intensive industries being especially affected (Chart 8).
In order to cope with these higher electricity costs than the European
average, Germany has been using European support schemes to reduce
the cost of electricity for businesses, particularly since the Merz go -

vernment took office in May 2025. The main scheme — the Industries-
trompreis14, made possible by the CISAF — has been approved by the
European Commission for the 2026–2028 period. While it is less bene-
ficial than the indirect carbon cost (ICC) offsetting included in electri -
city prices15, it has a broader scope, meaning that businesses ineligible
for the ICC can benefit from it. Other measures complement this targe-
ted support by addressing other components of the electricity bill. As a

result, the electricity tax has been set at the European minimum for
manufacturing sectors, while the gas-storage tax has been abolished.
The government is also subsidising grid fees, which should halve them.
Overall, according to our estimates based on 2024 prices, the final
price of electricity for these industries could fall by around a quarter
(to 116 EUR/MWh for industries with annual consumption of between
70 and 150 GWh, compared with a European average of 123 EUR/MWh)

(Chart 9). Germany’s advantage over Central and Eastern European
countries, where electricity costs are generally already higher, looks
set to increase.

However, even when taking all of the subsidies into account, the price
of electricity in Germany would remain higher than the levels seen in
France, Spain or Portugal (and even more so in the United States and
China).
FISCAL MEASURES: LIMITED ROOM FOR MANOEUVRE
There are a number of limitations to these European schemes. For
the country implementing them, lower electricity costs mean that

its industry becomes more competitive against industries from other
EU countries, but this does very little to address the disadvantage that
it faces compared with the United States and China. Therefore, this
measure will unlikely be enough to counteract the loss of market share
seen vis-à-vis the United States and China. In addition, the support
schemes are restrictive (conditional on investment in the low-carbon
transition) and are transitional. Finally, their cost is partly passed on

to the public purse or to consumers who do not receive this support
(in Germany, the cost of the new energy price support measures is
estimated at EUR 14.3 bn in 2026, or 0.3% of GDP16).
Some European manufacturers have proposed abolishing or reducing
the carbon tax as a solution to getting electricity prices down quickly.
In Germany, this tax accounted for 14% of the electricity price in the
first half of 2025, which is higher than the EU average (11%). However,

this proposal is already covered by the indirect carbon cost offsetting
mechanism for electricity-intensive industries exposed to carbon al -
lowance prices. In addition, while adjustments are being made — no -
tably to slow the rate at which allowances are reduced — it seems
highly unlikely that the scheme will be significantly challenged. It is
one of the cornerstones of Europe’s policy to combat climate change,
which has been effective thus far, as shown by the falling greenhouse

gas emissions from industry. More broadly, the gradual introduction of
a carbon border adjustment mechanism (CBAM) should mitigate com-
petition distortions caused by environmental practices17.
IN GERMANY AND THE REST OF EUROPE, HEAVY INVEST -
MENT IS DRIVING UP ELECTRICITY BILLS IN THE SHORT
TERM
In response to the challenge of the low-carbon transition and indus -
trial competitiveness, European governments must strike a balance
between decarbonising the electricity mix and ensuring a supply of

energy that is flexible enough (particularly from controllable sources)
to guarantee stable electricity systems. The integration of an increa -
sing proportion of intermittent renewable energy sources requires
massive investment in transmission and storage infrastructure (statio-
nary batteries). This expenditure, which places a long-term burden on
grid costs, reduces the scope for support schemes (particularly public
ones) to address competitiveness gaps. In Germany, the cost of the

transition is estimated at between EUR 122 bn, according to the go -
vernment (2.8% of GDP), and EUR 200 bn, according to the DIHK18 (4.6%
of GDP), by 2030–2035. If we take the ECB’s assumption19 that govern-
ments will cover around 17% of these investments, the annual public
16 The Industriestrompreis is estimated at around EUR 1.3 bn per year, the subsidy for network charges is projected to amount to EUR 6.5 bn in 2026, the abolition of the gas sur -

charge around EUR 3.4 bn, and the reduction in the electricity tax nearly EUR 3 bn per year. Furthermore, the ICC had cost EUR 2.3 bn in 2024 for costs incurred in 2023.
17 Jean-Luc Proutat, European Union: the carbon tax at the border in six questions, 6 October 2025.
18 German Chamber of Commerce and Industry, “Frontier economics, DIHK, 2025, New pathways for the energy transition (‘Plan B’)”.

20 Interest expenditure is expected to rise from 1.4% to 1.7% of GDP by 2030, due to rising refinancing rates.
21 Energy Institute, 2026, Statistical Review Home | Statistical Review of World Energy
22 Calls for tender are currently under way to build 12 GW of additional electricity generation capacity, with 10 GW covering gas-fired power stations (equivalent to one-third of the
existing gas-fired power stations) and 2 GW potentially allocated to batteries (accounting for 80% of existing large-capacity stationary batteries intended for the wholesale market).

expenditure burden of the transition in Germany would be between
0.5 and 0.8 percentage points of GDP by 2030. However, this budgetary
pressure comes at the same time as three other budgetary increases:
rising social expenditure linked to an ageing population; the necessary
increase to defence budgets to meet NATO’s target of 3.5% of GDP by
2029; and the rising cost of debt-servicing20.
Germany has already made significant progress in maximising its use

of renewable energy, putting it at the head of the pack in Europe. The
country generates 20% of Europe’s renewable electricity (solar and
wind) and accounts for 30% of installed storage capacity (BESS) 21, far
ahead of other European countries (ranking third globally). However,
despite this momentum, Germany’s dependence on gas has not yet
decreased. As a matter of fact, it is only partially offsetting the planned
closure of coal-fired power stations and the phasing out of nuclear

reactors. Furthermore, the intermittent nature of solar and wind power
could prolong this dependence in the short and medium term. In order
to ensure that the German electricity system is flexible, new invest -
ments in gas-fired power plants, supported by public subsidies, are
planned22.

SUBSIDIES ARE HELPING TO ADDRESS THE EMERGENCY
CAUSED BY ENERGY CRISES WITHOUT RESTORING THE
INTERNATIONAL COMPETITIVENESS OF ENERGY-INTEN-
SIVE INDUSTRIES
In response to the impact of energy crises on industrial competitive -
ness, European governments are finding themselves having to strike a
delicate balance between providing short-term support to industries

exposed to increased international competition and continuing — if
not, accelerating — energy transition policies which will result in hi -
gher costs for electricity-network users for several years to come.
Beyond direct economic support measures, which are difficult to sus -
tain long term, four priority areas are emerging:
1/ Demonstrating flexibility in implementing European directives in
order to factor in national specificities and the differing timeframes

between energy crises (which can lead to irreversible industrial conse-
quences) and reforms (which have a longer-term horizon). The relaxa-
tion of the European carbon tax mechanism 23 appears to be a step
towards this.
2/ Continuing efforts to decarbonise the electricity mix, which are still
the main vehicle for sustainably reducing electricity prices for Euro -
pean industries in the medium and long term.
3/ Optimising the use of electricity networks on the demand side, in

particular through smart grids, a flagship element of the Commission’s
COM (2026) 600 reform presented last July24.
4/ Coordinating European electricity networks in order to harness the
complementarities between national systems.
Article completed on 19 October 2026

Document 489: Who finances private credit in the United States_ What the Federal Reserve’s data reveal (2026-10-28)
